When I'm mopping after hours, I'll hear this... a man's voice. He'll say my name, or tell me I missed a spot. It sounds real clear, like he's just standing by the stairwell. But... when I look over, there's nobody there. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel your mood is perfectly normal, but you still hear that voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose. There was... a few weeks, maybe a month ago. I was feeling a bit better, I guess. Eating more. Getting through my shift without feeling so heavy. But... yeah, I still heard him. Just standing by the stairwell, saying my name. And... I still knew they were watching me on those hallway cameras. Tracking where I went. So... yeah. Even when I felt okay.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the time you've been having these experiences, would you say you have felt depressed or low in mood for most of that time, or have there been long stretches where your mood was generally okay?\n\nPatient: I don't know... mostly just low, I guess. Since I moved, it's just... been there. Most days. Just feeling heavy, tired... not caring about much. Even when the voice is bad, or I'm worried about the cameras... that heavy feeling is still there. Just in the background. Most of the time.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking with others, do you find that your thoughts get jumbled, or that people have trouble following what you are trying to say?\n\nPatient: ...I guess. Sometimes. Like... if my supervisor asks me something simple. Just asking which floors I finished. I try to tell him, but... I don't know, I just start talking about other things. The old bus route. Or... the garden plot, the church basement. I can hear myself doing it... just talking, and not getting back to the actual answer. So... yeah. I suppose they have trouble following me.\n\nDoctor: When you are having trouble staying on track during a conversation, do you ever find that your body feels frozen or that you become unable to move for periods of time?\n\nPatient: ...No, not frozen. I don't get stuck like that. I just... I don't know. Sometimes I just keep doing the same thing. Like wiping the same spot on the mirror, just... over and over. Or I'll be in the supply closet, just holding the broom. Just standing there holding it. It just feels like... I'm supposed to wait. Like I need a sign before I can go out and do the next thing. So I just wait. But I can move if I want to.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking in that scattered way, or when you find yourself repeating the same action over and over, does it feel like your mind is just blank and you don't really have a clear goal, or are you usually focused on the voice or the cameras when that happens?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. I guess... I'm not really sure what I'm focused on. It's just... happening. I'm not really thinking about the cameras or anything right then. Just... doing it, I guess. I don't really know how to explain it.\n\nDoctor: When you are in those periods where you find yourself repeating actions or feeling 'blank,' would you say that your speech is also consistently disorganized and difficult to follow, or is the difficulty in speaking something that happens at different times?\n\nPatient: ...I'm not really sure. I haven't noticed if they happen together. I just... I don't keep track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the voice and the cameras, do you feel like they are part of a specific plan or mission they have against you, or does it feel more like random things that just happen to you?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know about a mission. It's just... the new landlord. And some of the people in the building. I just... I know they're keeping tabs on me. Through the hallway cameras. They pass messages about me. People say the cameras are just for the front door, but... I know what they're doing. I just... get stuck on it.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, how long has it been since you first started noticing these changes—the heavy feeling, the voice, or the feeling that you were being watched?\n\nPatient: ...Maybe a year and a half. Even when I felt okay.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the time you've been having these experiences, would you say you have felt depressed or low in mood for most of that time, or have there been long stretches where your mood was generally okay?\n\nPatient: I don't know... mostly just low, I guess. Since I moved, it's just... been there. Most days. Just feeling heavy, tired... not caring about much. Even when the voice is bad, or I'm worried about the cameras... that heavy feeling is still there. Just in the background. Most of the time.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking with others, do you find that your thoughts get jumbled, or that people have trouble following what you are trying to say?\n\nPatient: ...I guess. Sometimes. Like... if my supervisor asks me something simple. Just asking which floors I finished. I try to tell him, but... I don't know, I just start talking about other things. The old bus route. Or... the garden plot, the church basement. I can hear myself doing it... just talking, and not getting back to the actual answer. So... yeah. I suppose they have trouble following me.\n\nDoctor: When you are having trouble staying on track during a conversation, do you ever find that your body feels frozen or that you become unable to move for periods of time?\n\nPatient: ...No, not frozen. I don't get stuck like that. I just... I don't know. Sometimes I just keep doing the same thing. Like wiping the same spot on the mirror, just... over and over. Or I'll be in the supply closet, just holding the broom. Just standing there holding it. It just feels like... I'm supposed to wait. Like I need a sign before I can go out and do the next thing. So I just wait. But I can move if I want to.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking in that scattered way, or when you find yourself repeating the same action over and over, does it feel like your mind is just blank and you don't really have a clear goal, or are you usually focused on the voice or the cameras when that happens?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. I guess... I'm not really sure what I'm focused on. It's just... happening. I'm not really thinking about the cameras or anything right then. Just... doing it, I guess. I don't really know how to explain it.\n\nDoctor: When you are in those periods where you find yourself repeating actions or feeling 'blank,' would you say that your speech is also consistently disorganized and difficult to follow, or is the difficulty in speaking something that happens at different times?\n\nPatient: ...I'm not really sure. I haven't noticed if they happen together. I just... I don't keep track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the voice and the cameras, do you feel like they are part of a specific plan or mission they have against you, or does it feel more like random things that just happen to you?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know about a mission. It's just... the new landlord. And some of the people in the building. I just... I know they're keeping tabs on me. Through the hallway cameras. They pass messages about me. People say the cameras are just for the front door, but... I know what they're doing. I just... get stuck on it.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, how long has it been since you first started noticing these changes—the heavy feeling, the voice, or the feeling that you were being watched?\n\nPatient: ...Maybe a year and a half. Like... a year and five months, I guess. It's just been building up since... since I had to leave my old apartment. Moving out of my old neighborhood. That's... that's when it started.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself standing still holding your broom or wiping the same spot, do you ever find that you are completely unaware of your surroundings or unable to respond to people talking to you during those moments?\n\nPatient: ...I'm not completely unaware. I know where I am. It's just... at work, sometimes I keep wiping the same patch of glass over and over. Or I'll stand in the supply closet just holding my broom. I just... it feels like I have to wait for the right sign before I can move on. So I just stay there.\n\nDoctor: When you are around other people, do you find it difficult to express your feelings or show reactions to things happening around you, such as appearing indifferent or 'flat' even when something good or bad occurs?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah. Even at work... just getting started on my cleaning route, it feels like... like pushing a loaded cart uphill. I just don't have it in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had people tell you that your behavior or the way you carry yourself seems strange, awkward, or out of place, even when you aren't feeling particularly low?\n\nPatient: ...No. Not that I know of. \n\nI mean... people don't really talk to me much anyway. Even when I felt okay.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the time you've been having these experiences, would you say you have felt depressed or low in mood for most of that time, or have there been long stretches where your mood was generally okay?\n\nPatient: I don't know... mostly just low, I guess. Since I moved, it's just... been there. Most days. Just feeling heavy, tired... not caring about much. Even when the voice is bad, or I'm worried about the cameras... that heavy feeling is still there. Just in the background. Most of the time.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking with others, do you find that your thoughts get jumbled, or that people have trouble following what you are trying to say?\n\nPatient: ...I guess. Sometimes. Like... if my supervisor asks me something simple. Just asking which floors I finished. I try to tell him, but... I don't know, I just start talking about other things. The old bus route. Or... the garden plot, the church basement. I can hear myself doing it... just talking, and not getting back to the actual answer. So... yeah. I suppose they have trouble following me.\n\nDoctor: When you are having trouble staying on track during a conversation, do you ever find that your body feels frozen or that you become unable to move for periods of time?\n\nPatient: ...No, not frozen. I don't get stuck like that. I just... I don't know. Sometimes I just keep doing the same thing. Like wiping the same spot on the mirror, just... over and over. Or I'll be in the supply closet, just holding the broom. Just standing there holding it. It just feels like... I'm supposed to wait. Like I need a sign before I can go out and do the next thing. So I just wait. But I can move if I want to.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking in that scattered way, or when you find yourself repeating the same action over and over, does it feel like your mind is just blank and you don't really have a clear goal, or are you usually focused on the voice or the cameras when that happens?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. I guess... I'm not really sure what I'm focused on. It's just... happening. I'm not really thinking about the cameras or anything right then. Just... doing it, I guess. I don't really know how to explain it.\n\nDoctor: When you are in those periods where you find yourself repeating actions or feeling 'blank,' would you say that your speech is also consistently disorganized and difficult to follow, or is the difficulty in speaking something that happens at different times?\n\nPatient: ...I'm not really sure. I haven't noticed if they happen together. I just... It's nice to meet you; could you tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess... I just don't feel like myself anymore. I don't really know. I barely talk to people lately. I just sort of... go flat, I guess. Quiet. And work... even just getting started on my cleaning route, it feels like pushing a loaded cart uphill. That's... that's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that other people around you do not seem to notice?\n\nPatient: ...I guess. Sometimes. Just... at work. When I'm mopping after hours, I'll hear this... a man's voice. He'll say my name, or tell me I missed a spot. It sounds real clear, like he's just standing by the stairwell. But... when I look over, there's nobody there. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel your mood is perfectly normal, but you still hear that voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose. There was... a few weeks, maybe a month ago. I was feeling a bit better, I guess. Eating more. Getting through my shift without feeling so heavy. But... yeah, I still heard him. Just standing by the stairwell, saying my name. And... I still knew they were watching me on those hallway cameras. Tracking where I went. So... yeah. Even when I felt okay.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the time you've been having these experiences, would you say you have felt depressed or low in mood for most of that time, or have there been long stretches where your mood was generally okay?\n\nPatient: I don't know... mostly just low, I guess. Since I moved, it's just... been there. Most days. Just feeling heavy, tired... not caring about much. Even when the voice is bad, or I'm worried about the cameras... that heavy feeling is still there. Just in the background. Most of the time.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking with others, do you find that your thoughts get jumbled, or that people have trouble following what you are trying to say?\n\nPatient: ...I guess. Sometimes. Like... if my supervisor asks me something simple. Just asking which floors I finished. I try to tell him, but... I don't know, I just start talking about other things. The old bus route. Or... the garden plot, the church basement. I can hear myself doing it... just talking, and not getting back to the actual answer. So... yeah. I suppose they have trouble following me.\n\nDoctor: When you are having trouble staying on track during a conversation, do you ever find that your body feels frozen or that you become unable to move for periods of time?\n\nPatient: ...No, not frozen. I don't get stuck like that. I just... I don't know. Sometimes I just keep doing the same thing. Like wiping the same spot on the mirror, just... over and over. Or I'll be in the supply closet, just holding the broom. Just standing there holding it. It just feels like... I'm supposed to wait. Like I need a sign before I can go out and do the next thing. So I just wait. But I can move if I want to.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking in that scattered way, or when you find yourself repeating the same action over and over, does it feel like your mind is just blank and you don't really have a clear goal, or are you usually focused on the voice or the cameras when that happens?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. I guess... I'm not really sure what I'm focused on. It's just... happening. I'm not really thinking about the cameras or anything right then. Just... doing it, I guess. I don't really know how to explain it.\n\nDoctor: When you are in those periods where you find yourself repeating actions or feeling 'blank,' would you say that your speech is also consistently disorganized and difficult to follow, or is the difficulty in speaking something that happens at different times?\n\nPatient: ...I'm not really sure. I haven't noticed if they happen together. I just... I don't keep track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the voice and the cameras, do you feel like they are part of a specific plan or mission they have against you, or does it feel more like random things that just happen to you?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know about a mission. It's just... the new landlord. And some of the people in the building. I just... I know they're keeping tabs on me. Through the hallway cameras. They pass messages about me. People say the cameras are just for the front door, but... I know what they're doing. I just... get stuck on it.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, how long has it been since you first started noticing these changes—the heavy feeling, the voice, or the feeling that you were being watched?\n\nPatient: ...Maybe a year and a half. Like... a year and five months, I guess. It's just been building up since... since I had to leave my old apartment. Moving out of my old neighborhood. That's... that's when it started.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself standing still holding your broom or wiping the same spot, do you ever find that you are completely unaware of your surroundings or unable to respond to people talking to you during those moments?\n\nPatient: ...I'm not completely unaware. I know where I am. It's just... at work, sometimes I keep wiping the same patch of glass over and over. Or I'll stand in the supply closet just holding my broom. I just... it feels like I have to wait for the right sign before I can move on. So I just stay there.\n\nDoctor: When you are around other people, do you find it difficult to express your feelings or show reactions to things happening around you, such as appearing indifferent or 'flat' even when something good or bad occurs?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah. I guess so. I just... I don't feel like myself anymore. I barely talk to anyone now. I just... go flat, like you said. Quiet. It's hard to even care about things happening around me. Even at work... just getting started on my cleaning route, it feels like... like pushing a loaded cart uphill. I just don't have it in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had people tell you that your behavior or the way you carry yourself seems strange, awkward, or out of place, even when you aren't feeling particularly low?\n\nPatient: ...No. Not that I know of. \n\nI mean... people don't really talk to me much anyway. I mostly just keep to myself. So... no, nobody has said anything like that to me.\n\nDoctor: When you are having those times where you find it hard to communicate, do you ever use words that don't make sense, or make up your own words that others wouldn't understand?\n\nPatient: ...I don't make up words. Not that I know of. \n\nBut... I guess sometimes I don't make sense. Like if my supervisor asks me a simple question... just asking which floors I finished. I try to answer him, but... I end up talking about the old bus route. Or the garden plot... the church basement. \n\nI can hear myself doing it. I know I'm not getting back to the answer he asked for. It just... comes out that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself laughing or giggling in situations where it didn't seem to make sense to others?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't think I laugh when I'm not supposed to. \n\nBut... I don't know. Sometimes I get stuck. Like at work... I'll just keep wiping the same spot on the glass. Over and over. Or... I'll just stand in the supply closet. Holding the broom. \n\nIt's just... I feel like I can't move yet. Like I have to wait for... for the right sign before I can go out and do the next thing. So I just stay there.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself stuck repeating the same action or talking about unrelated things like the garden or the bus route, does it always feel like you are doing those things because of the messages you think you are receiving from the cameras or the people in your building?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah. I guess so. \n\nI just... I get stuck on it. The new landlord, and... some of the people in my building. I just know they're keeping tabs on me. Through the hallway cameras. They pass messages about me. \n\nPeople tell me the cameras are just for the front door. But... I don't know. It doesn't feel that way.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you find yourself acting in those repetitive ways or having trouble with your speech when you aren't thinking about the landlord or the cameras at all?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. \n\nI guess... maybe. I'm not really sure. I just... I get stuck sometimes. That's all.\n\nDoctor: When you are at work, do you find that your dress, your personal hygiene, or your ability to keep yourself organized in your daily tasks has noticeably declined since these experiences started?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah. I guess things have slipped. \n\nMy supervisor... he started writing me up. For missing parts of my route. Or messing up the cleaning. I just... I couldn't keep the routine straight in my head anymore. \n\nI ended up going to that employee assistance thing they have. Just... because I couldn't keep up. And I haven't been doing much else, either. Stopped going to church. Haven't been fishing. Don't really check in on people like I used to. \n\nJust... hard to manage it all now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
